### T05fd9a0a3d254605c0ec69e8

Text hash 363a385bf2457bd6f7dd2c24b3ed7e8f79351cfed9507f00e319e2c3f5e5b376; absolute characters 83–632; files: jobs/clause-drafts/output/texts/cd-job-app-executive-orders-6107-aa2111c449.txt

```text
By virtue of the authority vested in me by section 204 of part II of the act entitled "AN ACT Making appropriations for the Legislative Branch of the Government for the fiscal year ending June 30, 1933, and for other purposes", approved June 30, 1932, Reuben Perry, superintendent of the Albuquerque School, New Mexico, Indian Service, Department of the Interior, who during the current month reaches the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, is hereby exempted from the provisions of that section until June 30, 1933.
```

### T06894b187f664cc7724caa70

Text hash d40de2500e443af711a82a981c74da5b9ea0b5db2a6c8c518d07ca7bb0b2b810; absolute characters 0–606; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6874-amendment-subdivision-vii-schedule-the-civil-service-rules.txt

```text
By virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by the provisions of the last sentence of the eighth paragraph of subdivision second of section 2 of the Civil Service Act of January 16, 1883 (ch. 27, 22 Stat. 403, 404), paragraph 6 of subdivision VII of schedule A of the civil-service rules is hereby amended to read as follows:

"6. Temporary clerks, earners, and laborers required for part-time or intermittent work in the Postal Service in connection with the present holiday or seasonal business from this date to February 1, 1935."

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

October 16, 1934.

```

### T2a6125cff8c32db3c07a73bd

Text hash 1f67a2dd2498bf11ce4a6ff233ff9146d2a8ae0c32f455261933f95962a03d1d; absolute characters 0–709; files: jobs/pinakes-proquest-ingest/output/texts/EO_4782.txt

```text
)xecuttve Orber
It is hereby ordered that the several executive departments and independent
Government establishments in the District of Columbia, including the Government
Printing Office and the Navy Yard and Stations, be closed on Saturday, December
24, 1927, and all clerks and other employees in the Federal service in the District
of Columbia, except those who may for special public reasons be excepted from the
provisions of this Order, or those whose absence from duty would be inconsistent
with the provisions of existing law, are hereby excused from duty on that day.
This Order is not to be deemed as establishing a precedent.
CALVIN COOLIDGE
THE WHITE HOUSE,
December 16, 1927.
[No. 47821
Trlu
97f
```

```text
The White House,

May 26, 1933.
```

### T4312025e1e2ef472e13d8f66

Text hash 7e86066099a298d99a3602480346c73632461f08d1af5a1f304780c6216d9666; absolute characters 0–1314; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-7247-exemption-nelson-h-darton-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt

```text
Whereas section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (5 U.S.C., sec. 715a), provides:

"On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch or service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the District of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in such service, notwithstanding any provision of law or regulation to the contrary: Provided, That the President may, by Executive Order, exempt from the provisions of this section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so requires * * * ";

And Whereas the public interest requires that Nelson H. Darton, senior scientist (geologist), Geological Survey, Department of the Interior, who, during the current month, will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, be exempted from the provisions of this section and continued in the service until January 1, 1937:

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by the aforesaid section, I do hereby exempt Nelson H. Darton from the provisions thereof and continue him in the service until January 1, 1937.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

December 10, 1935.

```

### T63ecf218efc638c49d7de2d9

Text hash 5740de9c59ced06b66e526eddcd76d61719b33714518e09b7944189ea68f579a; absolute characters 0–1373; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-7208-exemption-george-g-hedgcock-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt

```text
Whereas section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, ch. 314, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (5 U.S.C., sec. 692b), provides:

"On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch or service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the District of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in such service, notwithstanding any provision of law or regulation to the contrary: Provided, That the President may, by Executive Order, exempt from the provisions of this section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so requires * * * ";

And Whereas the public interest requires that George G. Hedgcock, senior pathologist, Bureau of Plant Industry, Department of Agriculture, who was exempted from compulsory retirement for a period of two years by Executive Orders No. 6338 of October 14, 1933, and No. 6862 of September 30, 1934, be further exempted from the provisions of this section and continued in the service until November 1, 1936:

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by the aforesaid section, I do hereby exempt George G. Hedgcock from the provisions thereof and continue him in the service until November 1, 1936.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

October 19, 1935.

```

```text
Whereas section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (5. U.S.C., sec. 715a), provides:

"On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch or service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the District of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in such service, notwithstanding any provision of law or regulation to the contrary: Provided, That the President may, by Executive Order, exempt from the provisions of this section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so requires * * *";

And Whereas the public interest requires that W. H. Moran, Chief of the Secret Service Division, Treasury Department, who was exempted from compulsory retirement for a period of two years by Executive Order No. 6647, of March 17, 1934, be further exempted from the provisions of this section and continued in the service until January 1, 1937:

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by the aforesaid section, I do hereby exempt W. H. Moran from the provisions thereof and continue him in the service until January 1, 1937.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

February 28, 1936.

```

### Ta707cc7c05e94890b0109c79

Text hash e8164f5e9405722df6c75484bce00e1a835a6c5afdabed7e6c18256e24c8d2f4; absolute characters 0–1282; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6663-exemption-luther-l-browning-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt

```text
Whereas section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932 (ch. 314, 47 Stat. 382, 404; 5 U.S.C., sec. 692 b), provides:

On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch or service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the District of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in such service, notwithstanding any provision of law or regulation to the contrary: Provided, That the President may, by Executive order, exempt from the provisions of this section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so requires * * *.

And Whereas the public interest requires that Luther L. Browning, linotype machinist in charge, Government Printing Office, who, during the current month will reach the retirement ago prescribed for automatic separation from the service, be exempted from the provisions of this section and continued in the service until April 1, 1935;

Now, Therefore, by virtue of the authority vested in me by the aforesaid statute, I do hereby exempt Luther L. Browning from the provisions thereof and continue him in the service until April 1, 1935.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

March 31, 1934.

```

### Tbfbe72a9636196f5890fc29e

Text hash 70d90d1421dee9ba6a699005b384b5a29eb2fdccdff709744a6cc9f72bddf2f7; absolute characters 0–831; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6516-excusing-federal-employees-the-district-columbia-from-duty-december.txt

```text
As an evidence of appreciation of the splendid service of the employees of the Government, most of whom have been working under exceptional pressure for the last 9½ months, it is hereby ordered that the several executive departments and independent Government establishments in the District of Columbia, including the Government Printing Office and the Navy Yard and stations, be closed on Saturday, December 23, 1933, and Saturday, December 30, 1933, and all clerks and other employees in the Federal service in the District of Columbia, except those who may for special public reasons be excepted from the provisions of this order, or those whose absence from duty would be inconsistent with the provisions of existing law, are hereby excused from duty on those days.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

December 20, 1933.

```

```text
Executive Other.
In order to effect the more expeditious construction, etc., of the proposed
buildings for the Boston, Massachusetts, Immigrant Station, the early comple-
tion of which in connection with war activities, constitutes a National Emergency
and by virtue of the provisions of the Act of Congrcss approved March 4, 191 7
( Stat. I192), entitled "An Act Making Provisions for the Naval Service for
the F'iscal Year ending June 30, 191 8, and for other purposes", whereby it is
provided "that in cases of National Emergency the President is authorized to
suspend provisions of law prohibiting more than eight hours labor in any one
day of persons enlgagedl upon work covered by contracts with the U nited States:
Provided further, that the wages of persons employed upon such contracts shall
be computed on a basic day rate of eight hours work, with over time rates
to be paid for at not less than time and one-half for all hours work in excess of
eight hours", I (10 hereby suspend the provisions of law prohibiting more than
eight hours of labo in anyv one day by persons engaged in the construction, etc.,
of the above mentioned proposed new structures. This order will take effect
from and after this date.
WOODROW WILSON
TImIII WITI'E [ousE,
27 August, 1918.
```

```text
"On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch or service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the District of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in such service, notwithstanding any provision ofjaw or regulation to the contrary: Provided, That the President may, by Executive Order, exempt from the provisions of this section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so requires * * *";

And Whereas the public interest requires that William H. Ramsey, Attorney, Criminal Division, Department of Justice, who was exempted from compulsory retirement for a period of one year by Executive Order No. 7278 of January 23, 1936, be further exempted from the provisions of this section and continued in the service until February 1, 1938;

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by the aforesaid section, I do hereby exempt William H. Ramsey from the provisions thereof and continue him in the service until February 1, 1938.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

January 29, 1937.

```

Taft), read in full in the held ProQuest replica scan (archival_replica_scan_pending_codebook, acceptable_as_original false) and checked against the page image I rendered from the held PDF (sha256 2e446a81...; one page; printed number 'No. 1547', date 'June 19, 1912', signature 'Wm H Taft'). The OCR layer is complete; its errors ('I19' for 19, a stray apostrophe after 'hereby', '[No. 1547-.1') do not affect the operative words. Single operative command: under section 2 of the act of June 19, 1912 limiting the hours of daily service of laborers and mechanics on federal work (the 1912 eight-hour act), the act's provisions and stipulations 'are hereby waived, until January first, nineteen hundred and fifteen, as to any contract or contracts entered into in connection with the construction of the Isthmian Canal.' First end: by its own terms on 1915-01-01. Under the owner's 2026-10-21 ruling the 'until' date is inclusive, so the waiver runs through the end of January 1, 1915; the alternative exclusive reading is that it stopped when that day began, leaving December 31, 1914 as its last day. Authority check: no held source shows section 2 of the 1912 act repealed or amended before 1915; the held text of EO 2663 (1917, jobs/wikisource-texts/output/texts/EO_2663.txt) invokes section 2 of the same act (37 Stat. 137), which corroborates that the authority still existed after this order's end. The act's own effective-date clause is not held; it does not affect the end. The order states its own date, so its end does not depend on when the canal construction contracts were completed.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier end is held. Candidates considered and not shown to extinguish the waiver: the Panama Canal Act of August 24, 1912 and EO 1885 (January 27, 1914, permanent organization of the Panama Canal) with EO 1888 (February 2, 1914, conditions of employment). The waiver attaches to contracts for canal construction, not to the Isthmian Canal Commission, and neither order's held summary or text names EO 1547 or the 1912 act's waiver; under the owner's rule a later general instrument ends a command only if something shows it extinguished that command, and nothing held does. The Panama Canal Act's text is not held. No extension: census titles, the WPA 1944 numbered list and the full text of all 2,750 ProQuest scan texts were searched on 2026-10-28 for Isthmian or Panama Canal orders touching eight-hour or hours-of-labor rules between 1912 and 1916; the only hits were EO 1547 itself, EO 2514 (January 15, 1917, Canal Zone leave rules, effective December 31, 1916) and EO 4459 (1926, Canal overtime pay), none of which names this waiver or acts before 1915. A citation search of every held order text for 'No. 1547' or 'Order 1547' found only Proclamation No. 1547 of 1919 (Scotts Bluff), a different instrument. The date is a fixed term of the waiver, not a deadline on another act.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"No independent command survives. The recital of section 2 of the 1912 act is authority, not a command. The waiver has no proviso imposing a separate continuing duty.\",\"savings_check\":\"Contracts entered into while the waiver ran could lawfully omit the eight-hour stipulations, and work done under them was lawful. Where such a contract continued after January 1, 1915, its terms stood as made; that is an accrued effect of a contract formed under the waiver, carried by the contract and the 1912 act, and it does not keep the order operative, because the order commands nothing after its stated date. Pay for hours worked flows from the contracts and statute, not from the order. No held source shows any contract affected after 1915; this residual is a disclosed qualification, not a finding.\",\"scope_check\":\"Whole order is one clause: a waiver for one class of contracts with one stated term. No partial amendment, extension or revocation of any part is held or found.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":[],"alignment":"scope_or_source_reconciliation_required","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:1547#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"537c53c6d0a9cea85bd02b9d633aad5d1bdb78a390184c5ff5e1ce5d6f2e2bc7","path":"jobs/pinakes-proquest-ingest/output/texts/EO_1547.txt","sha256":"ba9127150a42a4ea81ddbc6e2b04ec70390e5d19e738088aaeb0707a4792115a","state":"changed"},"sha256":"2e446a8148b78c1398a3d3a90c3cdce3887444eadc3abe69942b84d67afb198e","text_path":"jobs/pinakes-proquest-ingest/output/texts/EO_1547.txt","text_sha256":"537c53c6d0a9cea85bd02b9d633aad5d1bdb78a390184c5ff5e1ce5d6f2e2bc7"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"537c53c6d0a9cea85bd02b9d633aad5d1bdb78a390184c5ff5e1ce5d6f2e2bc7","path":"jobs/pinakes-proquest-ingest/output/texts/EO_1547.txt","sha256":"ba9127150a42a4ea81ddbc6e2b04ec70390e5d19e738088aaeb0707a4792115a","state":"changed"},"sha256":"2e446a8148b78c1398a3d3a90c3cdce3887444eadc3abe69942b84d67afb198e","text_path":"jobs/pinakes-proquest-ingest/output/texts/EO_1547.txt","text_sha256":"537c53c6d0a9cea85bd02b9d633aad5d1bdb78a390184c5ff5e1ce5d6f2e2bc7"}]},{"claim_sha256":"f8fb155600b1d7ee48ea7f750f4d3af482d58c4f10276680aeb523a927660218","current_canonical":false,"locations":[{"file_sha256":"e2ac1fabf9eee1f2168da47c8d29e7355401d6d9c27b1feb8d04577bdda0be9a","path":"research/excavator/claude-spent-probe-2026-10-28/packet/applied-v2/CLAIMS.proposed.jsonl","pointer":"line:1"}],"reported_material_gaps":[],"research_rationale":"EO 1547 (June 19, 1912, William H. It closes the executive departments and independent establishments in the District of Columbia, including the Government Printing Office and the Navy Yard and stations, on Saturday, December 24, 1927, and excuses federal employees in the District from duty that day, except those excepted for special public reasons or whose absence would conflict with existing law. No partial event is held or found.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:4782#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":["state_at_cutoff","first_end","later_events"],"scope_id":"EO:4782#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"1f67a2dd2498bf11ce4a6ff233ff9146d2a8ae0c32f455261933f95962a03d1d","path":"jobs/pinakes-proquest-ingest/output/texts/EO_4782.txt","sha256":"1f67a2dd2498bf11ce4a6ff233ff9146d2a8ae0c32f455261933f95962a03d1d","state":"match"},"sha256":"cf42ef50a95da80b5cb6109bcb93716da0d9556124f75635e213bbe19f807138","text_path":"jobs/pinakes-proquest-ingest/output/texts/EO_4782.txt","text_sha256":"1f67a2dd2498bf11ce4a6ff233ff9146d2a8ae0c32f455261933f95962a03d1d"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"1f67a2dd2498bf11ce4a6ff233ff9146d2a8ae0c32f455261933f95962a03d1d","path":"jobs/pinakes-proquest-ingest/output/texts/EO_4782.txt","sha256":"1f67a2dd2498bf11ce4a6ff233ff9146d2a8ae0c32f455261933f95962a03d1d","state":"match"},"sha256":"cf42ef50a95da80b5cb6109bcb93716da0d9556124f75635e213bbe19f807138","text_path":"jobs/pinakes-proquest-ingest/output/texts/EO_4782.txt","text_sha256":"1f67a2dd2498bf11ce4a6ff233ff9146d2a8ae0c32f455261933f95962a03d1d"}]}]}
